# FTESurf is a mod, and it is not portable to stock FTEQW: the Source movement
# module (engine/common/pm_source.c) and the hl2 plugin's .phy collision, VTF
# and VMT work only exist in the fork below.  Bump this file whenever a QC build
# needs an engine change; build.ps1 -Engine warns when your local engine HEAD
# does not match it.
#
# Deliberately not a git submodule: this repo does not build the engine, so a
# submodule would drag ~58 MB of C into every clone and imply a build
# relationship that does not exist.  A recorded SHA gives the same pin and
# degrades to a comment if it ever stops being maintained.

repo     https://github.com/ProtoAus/ftequakers
branch   main
commit   c433ba70b5cc15f69da6beb767c58fb9f517e199
# ^ The pin is the ENGINE_PATCHES.md work this QuakeC is written against, NOT the
#   commit a shipped binary was built from -- those drift, deliberately: patches
#   420-438 are all mod-side, so the engine source moved without any binary
#   needing to.  ftesurf64.exe carries its own stamp
#   (git-6891-patch-266-102-ga72183e6b = Patch 419) and the Linux drop's
#   BUILDINFO.txt carries its commit; release.ps1 compares THOSE two when -Linux
#   is passed (gate L2), and only warns about a mismatch with this line.  `patch`
#   below tracks ENGINE_PATCHES.md and moves with every entry, so the two lines
#   are not the same clock.  See AGENTS.md's release section before "fixing" this
#   by rebuilding: the rebuilt exe is a DIFFERENT binary and nothing records it.
# P1161 preserves published P1158 native and P1160 QC/backend work.

patch    1523

# Patch 658: the OpenGL renderer draws blended particles once a frame. GLBE_DrawWorld
# submitted the UNLITDECAL sort bucket twice (once before the lights, once after), and
# the script particle system's default alpha-blended shaders sort there: dust clouds,

# Patch 648: func_dustmotes is drawn the way Source draws it (c_func_dust.cpp). A mote is
# the entity's own colour, alpha, size range, life and speed, the same few pixels at any
# distance, faded by a sine through its life and to nothing at DistMax; the sprite's shape
# is computed per pixel, so a two-pixel mote is correct at any size. Each look on a map is
# an r_part block written at load and one inline shader draws them all; no more than
# Source's 2,048 are kept alive. Off OpenGL, off the scripted particle system or with
# vbsp_emit_motes 0 the old orbs draw and emit_list says which. func_dustcloud unchanged.
# csprogs only: no engine source, server QC, surfd, recorder, evidence, ranking, default
# or Build change (commit and tag above stay).
# Patch 649: sixty-four run lines can be ticked where eight could. The first eight ticked
# carry marks and numbers (hud_lines_marked); the lines in places 1..8 have names and are
# in the run graph; the rest are lines. A line in places 9..64 holds 8192 samples and a
# longer run is thinned to fit. Sixty-four lines add 1.9 ms to a frame's QC, less than
# eight did before Patch 645. Downloads for ticked online rows are paced to the server's
# twenty a minute, and a live run no longer repaints every ticked line four times a second.

# the lip more often than not. With the map loaded the viewer asks where the hull was
# last against a ramp face and stamps the mark there, between two samples. Where the map
# cannot say, the mark stays on the last contact sample (Patch 608). Replays and board
# lines; hud_lines_edge 0 turns it off; your own live line is unchanged.
# csprogs and ftesurf/cfg/default.cfg (the new switch): no engine source, server QC,
# recorder, evidence, ranking or Build change (commit and tag above stay).
# Patch 627: a run line marks the top of each arc and the bottom of each ride, with its
# speed, energy and time, and shows them by default (hud_lines_nums 2). A turn is
# stamped where vz crossed zero between two samples, and counts when it stands 8 units
# of height clear on both sides -- so a bottom ON a ramp is marked (none was), a wobble
# is not, and tops and bottoms alternate. The same marks on an imported run.
# csprogs and ftesurf/cfg/default.cfg (the label default): no engine source, server QC,
# recorder, evidence, ranking or Build change (commit and tag above stay).
# Patch 626: an imported Momentum run's line has ramp marks. A .mtv records no ramp
# contact, so the viewer infers it from the samples (a push up between two ticks in the
# air; the fall a tick is read off the file) and says so: `~` before a ramp edge's
# number, `inferred` in the replay's status, `ramp: no plane` on the strafe bar. The
# Segments column gains its ramp rows. 5,257 of 5,281 imports; the other 24 say
# `unavailable` and why (one of them: a run over 200,000 samples, which is not inferred
# because a replay opens in one QC call with a fixed budget). No recording is rewritten
# and the plane is not inferred.
# csprogs only: no engine source, server QC, recorder, evidence, ranking, default or
# Build change (commit and tag above stay).
# Patch 624 (published after 625; `patch` does not move down): a ticked board line is

# the line nearest the eye and 2 s behind it (hud_lines_order; 0 is everything within
# hud_watch_path_far, as before), so a later part of the route no longer shows through a

# PATCH 492: current Momentum's collision on `pm_fixrampbugs 2`, which default.cfg
# now sets: Source's brush test (both-in-front early-out, DIST_EPSILON-adjusted
# enter and leave, NEVER_UPDATED, Source's tie) and the slide-bug rule (a fraction-0
# hit goes to the crease); triangles and props keep their test.  surf_voyager

# PATCH 493: MOD-SIDE ONLY -- no engine file is touched and no engine builtin is used,
# so nothing here needs a rebuild and it is version-skew safe against the older Pi
# engine.  A usercmd's move triple reaches the server with NO RANGE TEST ANYWHERE: the
# engine's server side is a bare `sv_player->xv->movement[0] = ucmd->forwardmove;` and
# the only client-side bound is cl_input.c's `bound(-32768, ..., 32767)`, a wire bound
# and not a plausibility one.  The physics never needed one (PMSrc_WishDir clamps
# wishspeed to pms_maxspeed), which is exactly why an impossible value is free evidence.
# And the corpus says the impossible value is what this class of cheat writes: two
# samples in C:/FTESurf-private/Cheats fly a body back to a recording's start with
# `cmd->forwardmove = dist;` -- a DISTANCE, in the thousands on any map that respawns
# away from its start.  SV_MoveBoundFrame now compares each command's largest |axis|
# against `run_movebound` (2000, 0 = off, latched per map beside run_startcap so a
# file cannot state one bound and judge its rows against another), counts the ones over
# it, keeps the largest value the attempt sent at all, and sets TF_MOVEOOR (262144).
# SV_RecOpen states the bound as a `movebnd` header key, `cmd timer` prints
# `movebnd: bound N over N worst N moveoor N`, and reccheck.py cross-checks the bit
# against the file's own `in` rows in BOTH directions -- rows over the stated bound
# with the bit clear is a FAULT, the converse a NOTE, and no key an abstention on the
# `proprule` precedent.  MARKER ONLY, on the SV_RecCounts precedent: no class, not in
# TF_UNCERT, nothing demoted, because THREE HONEST ROUTES PASS THE BOUND -- the four
# move cvars are plain archived cvars (cl_movespeedkey's engine default is 2.0 where
# default.cfg ships 1, so +speed sends 900), mouse-strafe adds `m_side * mouse_x` per
# command, and A GAMEPAD WITH +speed HELD IS UNBOUNDED, found by reading rather than
# measuring: in_generic.c's IN_MoveJoystick scales jstrafe by `360 * cl_movespeedkey`
# and then multiplies by cl_forwardspeed without re-normalising, reaching 450*360 and
# stopped only by the wire bound.  A demotion on this axis would land on an honest
# gamepad player first -- the Patch 305 failure mode.  The bound's value is a
# judgement over one corpus: 1,342,728 axis values in 63 fleet runs peak at EXACTLY
# 450 (default.cfg's own move cvars), and the only values above it in 5,571 local files
# are 11,460 in three harness files.  Verified: test_reccheck 306 checks 0 failed (was
# 295), a 266-file corpus sweep unchanged at 169 files with faults and no new
# `movebnd` finding, and tools/p493move.py 26 checks 0 failed -- control `bound 2000
# over 0 worst 450 moveoor 0`, subject at cl_forwardspeed 5000 `over 120 worst 5000
# moveoor 1` growing to `over 283`, the closed file carrying `movebnd 2000` and
# `flags 395013` with reccheck reading `284 of 1938 over 2000 (worst 5000)`, both
# tamper directions faulting, and `+set run_movebound 0` giving five samples of
# `bound 0 over 0 worst 0 moveoor 0` with a file that carries no key at all.  THE
# PREDICTION THAT FAILED WAS THE ONE THAT MATTERED: this harness was built to grade a
# Multi-Session park on the argument that SV_MsEligible gates on elapsed time only, and
# the park produced a closed file reading `flags 0` beside 284 rows over the bound --
# SV_RecFlagLine reserves a fixed width at open and only SV_RecClose/SV_RecKeepEvidence
# seek back to rewrite it, so a marker set after the open is absent from a parked file
# and from a save-state prefix.  The first cut of the cross-check FAULTED that file,
# i.e. accused a recording the writer never finished; it is now gated on `end` like
# every other header-bit cross-check, and the arm finishes its run instead (b88fin's
# route, reachable because the zone scan is a server-side point test that a noclip
# flight does cross).  NOT VERIFIED: no sample in the corpus runs against this engine,
# so the subject is an ordinary archived cvar set to 5000, which measures the
# observation and not the accusation.

# PATCH 491: a remote server may no longer turn a connected client into a cluster
# master.  This was the LAST UNARMED item in the client-security audit, and arming it
# DISPROVED the audit's own note that the route "needs a local/listen server first".
# `mapcluster`, `ssv` and `ssv_all` are registered by SV_InitOperatorCommands with no
# restriction level, and a CLIENT runs that too because it can host -- so a remote
# server's stufftext reached MSV_MapCluster_Setup, whose first unconditional act is
# CL_Disconnect(NULL), after which the victim's process became a cluster master with a
# landing map of the server's choosing, and the subserver fork that follows is
# CreateProcessW.  Measured: the victim was dropped (server-side `Client "Proto"
# removed`) on a plain client that had only ever connected to somebody else's server.
# THE GATE SITS IN MSV_MapCluster_Setup AND NOT IN THE COMMAND HANDLER, because the
# function has TWO command legs and gating one is not gating the command (Patch 485):
# the other is SV_Map_f's auto-offload, which a server reaches in two steps since
# `sv_autooffload` is a plain CVARD with no CVAR_NOTFROMSERVER.  Naming Cmd_Argv(0)
# makes the coverage observable -- the gate prints `mapcluster` on one leg and `map` on
# the other.  `ssv`/`ssv_all` are deliberately NOT gated: the write path is inert
# without a cluster and the no-argument listing prints only to the victim's own
# console, so it stays available as a harmless reachability probe.  Driven over a
# matched pair differing only in sv_cluster.c, four runs (two legs x control/subject),
# 4 predictions each, all met on all four.  Client-affecting but SERVER-side code; no
# QC and no cfg depends on it, so no lobby deploy is implied.  Arm is private
# (poc/p491/).

# PATCH 489: `allskins` no longer copies a server-supplied string past the end of its
# buffer.  `Skin_AllSkins_f` was a bare `strcpy (allskins, Cmd_Argv(1))` into
# `char allskins[128]`, with `qwskin_t skins[256]` declared immediately after it, and
# the command has no restriction level and no insecure check -- so a remote server's
# stufftext reached an unbounded write of a string it chose.  Now `Q_strncpyz`: THE
# BOUND, NOT A GATE, because forcing skins is a legitimate QuakeWorld server feature
# and this command must stay server-reachable.  Measured with the canary build the
# audit had asked for and never had: a guard word at offset 128 came back holding the
# argument's own bytes (`0x62626262`) and the client died in the next statement, while
# the bounded build left it intact at 15, 127 and 300 characters.  Driven over FOUR
# builds -- the canary pair as the detector and the real pair as the regression arm,
# which behaved identically -- 5 predictions, all met on all four.  The impact class
# stays deliberately narrow: the crash belongs to the instrumented layout and the
# uninstrumented build survived the same argument.  CLIENT-ONLY, no lobby deploy
# implied.  Arm is private (poc/p489/).

# PATCH 488: a server may no longer name the DLL the client's renderer loads.  The
# subrenderer token that `setrenderer "gl <name>"` supplies is a DLL NAME --
# GLInitialise hands it to Sys_LoadLibrary and the Vulkan path hands it straight to
# LoadLibrary -- and R_BuildRenderstate stored an explicit token with NO filter while
# the path filter beside it ("that would be too exploitable") ran only on the
# gl_driver fallback branch.  `setrenderer` is registered with no restriction level,
# so a remote server's stufftext reached it and got native code running inside the
# client process: driven with a canary DLL whose DllMain wrote a marker line and then
# declined the load.  Now refused whole from an insecure caller -- WHOLE, not screened
# for slashes, because a bare name is also a DLL name and LoadLibrary resolves it
# through the standard search order, which includes the client's own cwd.  The
# renderer NAME is untouched, so a server may still ask for gl/vk/sw, and a local
# caller keeps everything (measured: the local token still reached the loader on both
# builds).  Driven subject/control, 5 predictions, all met on both.  CLIENT-ONLY, no
# lobby deploy implied.  Arm is private (poc/p488/).  NOT gated: the archived
# `vid_renderer` boot route, which is a local actor's own setting and whose only
# server-side link was item 8, closed by Patch 483 -- see BACKLOG.

# PATCH 487: a teamplay trigger alias now runs at the ALIAS's own execlevel, which
# is what makes Patch 481 a boundary again.  TP_ExecTrigger looked the alias up with
# Cmd_AliasExist(name, RESTRICT_LOCAL) and ran its BODY as raw text at LOCAL, so the
# alias's own `execlevel` -- RESTRICT_SERVER for anything a server stuffed -- was
# never consulted.  One stuffed `alias f_newmap "<anything>"` therefore gave a remote
# server that anything back with the USER's privileges, and because Cmd_IsInsecure()
# reads Cmd_ExecLevel, every gate 481/483/485 added read 29 for it.  Now resolved
# through a new Cmd_AliasExecLevel at both executing sites (TP_ExecTrigger and
# TP_SearchForMsgTriggers) and both of TP_ExecTrigger's branches; all six
# Cmd_AliasExist call sites were read, and the other four pass the alias NAME to the
# dispatcher, which already applies its own level.  Driven subject/control, 6
# predictions, all met on both: the planted alias's gated command printed its listing
# 4x on the control and was refused 3x on the subject, with the local-alias
# regression control and the direct-stuff control IDENTICAL on both.  Engine-side,
# CLIENT-ONLY -- a dedicated server never runs TP_ExecTrigger, so no lobby deploy is
# implied and no QC build depends on it.  Arm is private (poc/p487/) because it is a
# working recipe for a hole every shipped engine still has.

# PATCH 485: gamedir, mapfrom and fs_restart refuse a server's stufftext.  mapfrom
# was the finding -- it re-queues TWO commands at the caller's level, Patch 481
# already refused the fs_useaddons leg, and the `map "@<argv(1)>/<argv(2)>"` leg
# STILL RAN: measured, a stuffed `mapfrom ftesurf bhop_eazy` printed SpawnServer, so
# a remote server made the client host a map.  fs_restart is a DoS (the mod's own
# sv_player.qc calls it the most expensive command in the build).  Engine-side, no QC
# build depends on it, and no QC or shipped cfg calls any of the three.
# NOTE: this pin's `patch` moved 483 -> 484 -> 485 but 484 has no note here; that
# entry is the other session's to write (engine b31da1437).

# PATCH 483: saveconfig, setinfoblob and fs_changegame refuse a server's stufftext.

# an else-if ON the insecure test, so an exempt path never reached it).  Engine-side,
# no QC build depends on it.  THE MACRO TRAP IT COST AN ARM TO FIND is in AGENTS.md:
# `Cmd_IsInsecure() && !Cmd_FromGamecode()` means "block QC, admit the server".

# PATCH 482: the JSON reader decodes \uXXXX from the source, not the destination,
# and pairs a low surrogate across the whole of U+DC00..U+DFFF (it stopped at DE00,
# so U+1F600 -- D83D DE00 -- never paired).  Reaches QC's jsondecode, i.e. every
# name and map title off the web board.  Engine-side; no QC build depends on it, so
# a client still on the 467 engine draws the old wrong text rather than failing.

# PATCH 346 IS ENGINE-ONLY, and the first half of FTESURF-REC 9: SV_RunCmd
# snapshots the 70 values the mover is handed (movevars, pm_type, hull,
# gamespeed, trace cvars, PMSRC_VERSION) with an epoch that bumps on change, a

# run_physcrc run_portalx run_portalorg run_portalvel) and per-client infokeys
# (*pmpinN, *pmstateN).  `pm_pin` prints it.  Nothing reads it until QC build 87.
# Needs -Full (pmove.h, server.h).  cfg/test/p346pin.cfg.

# PATCH 345 IS ENGINE-ONLY (sv_ccmds.c): pm_recsim rounded the `in` row's
# %.4f angle back to a wire short by TRUNCATION, so ~46% of replayed rows gave
# the mover a yaw one step (0.0055 deg) low.  Rounding recovers every short;
# velocity-error p90 falls 25-30x on real runs.  qcbuild stays 83.
# cfg/test/p345angle.cfg.

# PATCH 344 IS ENGINE-ONLY (sv_ccmds.c), the first of build 85's two engine
# follow-ups below: pm_recsim now replays `ride` (keyed to the `in` row it
# precedes, in SV_BaseVelocityFrame's order) and `inend` (the final move is
# run), and its v7 "nothing imposed state" line is narrowed to what v7 can
# express.  Measurement command only; qcbuild stays 83.  cfg/test/p344ride.cfg.
# (First committed as 343, which a concurrent mod-side patch had just taken.)

# PATCH 343 IS MOD-SIDE ONLY (no engine change): the replay's segment column
# draws its OWN rows.  rec_wt_sq* captured eight columns and never gained
# seq_eend (build 65) or seq_sub (build 66), so every replayed row took its
# absolute and its Jump/Bhop word from whatever the build pass left in the live
# ring -- the run's last rows against whichever rows were on screen.  Two more
# capture columns, written in Watch_SeqRow and read in Watch_SeqApply; the
# absolute is the file's and the zero stays the viewer's, which is the build-39
# approximation Watch_Splits already documents.  Fixture cfg/test/b86c.cfg.

# PATCH 342 IS MOD-SIDE ONLY (no engine change): five UI fixes, four of them
# regressions.  Patch 341's CSQC_ChatSay swallowed `say <text>` as well as bare
# `say`, so `say !r` / `!m` / `!s N` opened a prefilled draft and sent nothing --
# default.cfg binds r and m to those, so the restart keys were dead and six
# cfg/test fixtures had been driving no runs.  It now declines a non-empty
# argument and lets CL_Say send it (that one quote pair is the one SV_Say strips,
# so `!s 2` keeps its number); bare `say` still opens the draft.  `-showscores`
# closes a MOUSE2-pinned board instead of only clearing the hold -- MOUSE2 is
# +jump, so jumping while peeking used to pin it by accident.  The segment column
# stamps the energy anchor PER ROW (new seq_eref, a tenth parallel array, plus a
# `rowref` save line and the replay park pair, which also regains the seq_eend /
# seq_sub it had dropped since builds 65/66) so committed rows stop restating
# themselves every time a jump re-bases the anchor.  Air rows (Jump/Bhop/Air) are
# mint green.  The running clock is coloured by your pace against your own PB,
# latched from the stage/checkpoint deltas the client already gets, behind
# `hud_timer_pace` -- silent on public lobbies, which set `lobby_nopb 1`.
# Engine tree unchanged since 341; qcbuild stays 83 until the next Build commit.

# PATCH 341 ADDS ONE ENGINE HOOK: CL_Say offers the whole command to a new
# name-bound csqc globalfunction CSQC_ChatSay(args, team) before it sends
# (zqtp.c), so the console's `say` opens the mod's chat draft instead of the
# corner input.  Name-bound like the rest: old engine + new csprogs is never
# asked, new engine + old csprogs has nobody to ask -- both directions fall
# back to the engine prompt.  Everything else in 341 is mod-side: quote-free
# chat submission via a quoted cvar, the always-reserved draft line, hold-TAB
# / pin-MOUSE2 scoreboard, and `setinfo lobbyrgb` exact body colour with the
# nearest palette index kept for particles and chat tc (`lrgb` publishes the
# exact triple).

# PATCH 340 IS MOD-SIDE ONLY (no engine change): player customization phase 2.
# The menu gates launch/join on a default name (SCREEN_NAME); the server rolls
# a random free avatar colour per connection and publishes it as `tc` (the
# engine's own chat-name colour key, so chatbox AND console names match the
# body) plus `lc`, and the run-filing hash as `*phash` -- forceinfokey, because
# a stuffed `set` is Cvar_LockFromServer and cvar_string reads the latch.  New
# csprogs: cl_chat.qc (CSQC_Parse_Print -> stacking fading bottom-left chatbox;
# once defined the hook takes ALL prints, so other levels are re-printed),
# cl_avatar.qc (`hud_player` customizer: colour/trail swatches, fx chips, hash
# line), cl_players.qc (room list drawn INSIDE the board's sui bracket, voice
# thumbnails, local silence list; spectate stubbed by decision).  qcbuild
# stays 83 until Build 84.

# PATCH 338 IS MOD-SIDE ONLY (no engine change): the IO mirror.  cl_triggers
# now replicates the Enable/Disable half of entity I/O -- touch edges of
# predicted slots and event-only trigger_multiples, logic_auto OnMapSpawn on
# schedule -- with a demotion fixpoint that keeps everything else (relays,
# buttons, timers, OnTrigger/OnJump, delays, Toggle, All-variants,
# player-movement writes) server-authoritative.  Gate state rides a
# sequence-keyed ring committed at chain start, with persistent last-overlap
# stamps separating continuous stays from warp-ins.  mom_training's

# locally; the backtrack setspeed runs its full Enable/Disable cycle with
# zero corrections.  tools/seed_csprogs.py exists because a stale
# downloads/csprogsvers cache silently ran two rounds of OLD code against the
# fix.  qcbuild stays 83 until Build 84.

# PATCH 336 IS THE FILTER MIRROR.  Patch 335 skipped every filtered trigger;
# the server's own essay says missing and unimplemented filters PASS (Source
# fails open), and the two implemented classes are lump-static except for the
# player's runtime targetname.  cl_triggers.qc now evaluates filters
# three-valued from the lump: static PASS predicts, UNKNOWN (name filters --
# futile's anti-backtrack) and FAIL stay server-only.  bhop_arcane recovers
# its 16 filter_standing triggers; futile correctly keeps all 35.  Mod QC

# PATCH 335 MOVES THE TRIGGERS INTO PREDICTION.  Phase 1 of playing on the
# client: CSQC_PredictPlayerMove runs the stateless Source triggers
# (teleport, push, setspeed) inside the client's own usercmd replay, so a

# PATCH 334 TEACHES THE PICKER TO WAIT.  The join reply's state/wait fields

# BUILD 85 IS QC-ONLY -- the anti-cheat line, not the Patch 329-341 workstream
# above.  FTESURF-REC 8 adds the velocity carrier (`ride <pk> <mt> arm|pay

# inspector work claims 84 in its own essays and is still uncommitted.

# TWO ENGINE FOLLOW-UPS IT IDENTIFIED AND DID NOT MAKE:
#   1  pm_recsim (sv_ccmds.c:4419-4420) still prints the v7 "nothing imposed
#      state on this run" claim that E5 measured false -- wrong on 181 of 536
#      zoned maps.  Narrow it to "nothing THIS FORMAT CAN EXPRESS"; from v8 the
#      strong form is earned.  Two strings.
#   2  pm_recsim does not CONSUME the record, so a re-simulation still cannot
#      cross a ride.  That is the real prerequisite for the Phase 3 verifier.

# AND ONE IT PROVED IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKED LIKE.  The plan's item 5 ("a stage
# transition writes no `warp`") is NOT a missing call site.  surf_kitsune moves
# the player through 18 linked_portal_door entities, and a portal crossing is
# committed by PM_PlayerTracePortals (pmove.c:156) INSIDE the mover -- outside
# SV_RecWarp's stated boundary by construction, so the trailer's warp count is
# correct rather than short.  Patch 205 removed the touch handler these doors
# had, so there is no QC site to hang a record on.  The fix publishes the

# to QC beside run_movetick, on the Patch 240/325 precedent: an engine patch.
# Worth doing -- a portal is a discontinuity a verifier cannot otherwise explain.

# PATCH 329 IS ENGINE-ONLY -- qcbuild STAYS 83.  r_voidfog lives entirely in

# BUILD 83 MOVES qcbuild, AND THE 327 NOTE BELOW DOES NOT APPLY TO IT.  The two
# halves are a writer and a reader: QC build 83 writes a new record type (`warp`)
# and a new header key (`startjit`), and Patch 328 is what reads them.
#
# NEITHER HALF NEEDS THE OTHER TO RUN, so this is a pin and not a hard
# requirement -- a build-83 server on a Patch 327 engine writes the records
# perfectly well, and pm_recsim is a measurement command no player's install
# ever reaches.  It is pinned anyway because a recording that CARRIES warp
# records and a harness that CANNOT SEE them is exactly the pair that produces a
# confident wrong answer: before 328 the parser had no else-branch, so an
# unknown record was not skipped-with-a-count, it was invisible.
#
# (The 327 note, kept because its reasoning stands for that patch:)
#
# qcbuild STAYED 82 for 327 AND THAT WAS NOT AN OVERSIGHT.  Patch 327 adds a measurement
# command (pm_recsim) and nothing else; no QC build needs it, and a player's
# install runs identically with or without it.  `commit` moves anyway, because
# this file's only automated consumer is build.ps1 -Engine's "HEAD does not match"
# warning, and leaving the SHA behind a committed patch would make that warning
# mean "somebody committed something" instead of "there is uncommitted work in
# the tree".

# NOTE, CORRECTED 2026-09-14: the long-standing caveat here -- "`commit` is still
# patch 266's SHA because everything from 267 up is applied in the working tree
# but NOT YET COMMITTED" -- is FINALLY OBSOLETE.  Patches 267-323 are committed
# on `engine-patches`, and `commit` above now pins the real SHA rather than the
# last tag's.  `tag` is still patch-266 because no newer tag has been cut;
# `git describe --tags` reads patch-266-N-g<sha>.  If `build.ps1 -Engine` warns
# that HEAD does not match this file again, it means a NEW uncommitted patch is
# in the tree -- which is now information rather than noise.

#        velocity against a network field that is not maintained for your own

#        stand still at high framerates.  With 269 in, the workaround build 47

#        measurement had to be shippable to the machine that reproduces it.
#   269c engine/client/cl_pred.c AND engine/client/client.h (header change --
#        rebuild with -Full).  269 was too big a hammer and regressed the

#        reported VELOCITY unblended, so energy -- which combines them -- read
#        two different instants and climbed ~4 units over a jump that conserves

#   271  plugins/hl2/mod_vbsp.c: VBSP_MarkLeaves refused to compute a PVS for
#        any RECURSED view, and VBSP_PrepareFrame answers a missing PVS by
#        drawing every surface in the world model.  Mirrors/portals/water hand
#        down a real PVS and were fine; the 3D SKYBOX hands down nothing, so on
#        every Source map with a sky_camera the whole map was drawn a second
#        time each frame with no PVS, no frustum and no area test.  On
#        surf_tensor2 that pass emitted 24,762 surfaces -- exactly
#        dmodel_t[0].numfaces -- where the sky_camera's own cluster admits 30.
#        Fixed by computing the PVS and poisoning the shared cache, which is
#        what q1bsp.c already does.  236 -> 323 fps at the spawn, Opaque
#        Batches 1500 -> 288us, and the main view unchanged to the mesh.
#        QC build 47 does not need it -- it is a pure rendering fix and moves

#   273  engine/client/cl_screen.c + engine/server/{sv_main.c,sv_phys.c,server.h}
#        and plugins/hl2/mod_vbsp.c (HEADER CHANGE -- rebuild with -Full).
#        Three things, in the order they were found:
#          - r_speeds' "Framerate (refresh only)" meant it: RSPEED_SERVER is
#            bracketed separately from RSPEED_TOTALREFRESH, so on a listen server
#            the headline number omitted the whole server.  323 fps on the
#            surf_tensor2 spawn was really 204.7.  Now prints both.
#          - the listen server's cost was 98% one function: VBSP_HeadnodeVisible,

#          - VBSP_MarkLeaves scanned all 19,846 leafs to light up the 82 the PVS
#            admits, and its single vis cache was evicted every frame by the 3D
#            skybox view.  Leaves are now bucketed by cluster at load, and the

#        census is byte-identical before and after (506 / 20 meshes) and

#        not culled before.  Moves no run time: renderer, reporting and the

#        rebuild with -Full).  DRAWFLAG_NODEPTHTEST (0x1000) for R_BeginPolygon
#        and R_AddTrisoup, mapping to the BEF_FORCENODEPTH every backend already
#        honoured but no QC could ask for.  QC BUILD 52 NEEDS IT: cl_sprites.qc's

#        THE THING 279 RESOLVED AND COULD NOT DRAW: comshieldwall.vtf is 31
#        frames and its proxy chain picks one by DISTANCE, clamp(dist*0.2-24,
#        0,30), so a combine shield should get DENSER as you approach.  We drew
#        frame 0 forever, which is why the web got brighter and never denser.
#        The way in is that the VMT points $basetexture and $texture2 at the
#        same file AND drives $frame and $frame2 from two identical Clamp
#        proxies -- so it is one 2DArray read twice at one layer, which is
#        vmt/animated's proven one-pass one-sampler shape.  #FRAMES, taken only
#        for a same-texture material; 7 of the library's 48 UnlitTwoTexture
#        materials qualify, covering 42 of surf_tensor2's 44 shield faces.
#        Off switch hl2_twoframes.  QC build 54 does not need it.
#   287  NOT MINE -- a concurrent session's, a portal's far side culled by the

#        THE ONE THING THAT MATTERS TO A QC BUILD: the fix is behind cvar

#        read "wayy to blue": the PCF genuinely is blue and in Source the WHOLE

#   290  engine/gl/gl_rmain.c (two hunks), plugins/hl2/mat_vmt.c,
#        plugins/hl2/mod_vbsp.c (a census line).  No header change; -Engine.
#        288 SHIPPED A BLACK SCREEN AND THIS IS WHY IT COULD.  Its generated
#        shader script began with "{".  That is right for R_RegisterShader,
#        whose Shader_DefaultScript (gl_shader.c:7379-7391) requires and strips
#        exactly one -- and wrong for a material loader, because
#        Shader_LoadMaterialString (:8386-8390) strips nothing and starts
#        parsing INSIDE the body.  So the brace opened a PASS, `program` bound
#        pass->prog instead of shader->prog, and the console said only
#        "unexpected indentation".
#        The stage then drew nothing, and drawing nothing is NOT harmless:
#        R_RenderPostProcess allocates a render target (:2639), never clears it,
#        and returns it unconditionally (:2659) -- so a failed post-process
#        stage costs the whole picture, not just its own effect.  290 therefore
#        also GATES the stage on the shader having a program AND a pass, so any
#        future failure is ungraded rather than blank.  Verified: pointing
#        r_colourcorrection at a name that cannot resolve leaves the picture
#        normal and prints one warning.
#        WITH IT WORKING, the grade moves 91.7% of the frame against a 0.4%
#        same-setting control on surf_tensor2, and near-white goes
#        (251.7,253.1,253.6) -> (232.9,239.2,251.9) against a prediction of
#        (234.2,239.8,253.0) from the LUT's white point at weight 0.40.
#        A TRAP THAT INVALIDATED EVERY EARLIER POST-PROCESS MEASUREMENT:
#        screenshot_mega forces an FBO (cl_screen.c:2941-2943), and gl_rmain.c
#        :2841-2845 clears RDF_ALLPOSTPROC whenever dofbo is set.  A mega
#        screenshot therefore contains NO post-processing at all -- no gamma, no
#        fxaa, no r_postprocshader, no grade.  Use plain `screenshot` for
#        anything that lives after the scene.
#        Also: $ignorez used to emit `nodepth` at the shader top level, where it
#        is not a key at all (it is a PASS key, gl_shader.c:4838).  It never did
#        anything; it is now counted and declined instead of printing a warning
#        that reads like a typo.
#   268 C  engine/client/render.h + engine/gl/shader.h (HEADER CHANGE -- rebuild
#        with -Full), engine/gl/{gl_shader.c,gl_vidcommon.c,gl_backend.c,

#        Per-pixel relief on bumped props -- BUILT, MEASURED, AND NOT ADOPTED.
#        Every switch ships 0, so this is inert in the deployed build; it is in
#        the tree because the uniform, the ambient-cube fill and the GLSL are
#        prerequisites for the redesign named below.  A bumped VertexLitGeneric
#        with no $envmap (surf_fantasy's island rocks) sampled its normal map
#        for nothing, so it drew flat where Source shows moss relief.  The fix
#        tried was Source's six-face ambient cube per pixel applied as a RATIO
#        over VRAD's bake -- and a ratio of a cube against ITSELF at a slightly
#        different normal is pinned near 1: measured 0.00% of the frame on two
#        rocks filling the centre, and 0.9941 mean ratio (0.76 of 255 levels,
#        0.02% of pixels over the 8-level threshold) computed off the real
#        normal map under a synthetic hard top-lit cube, the best input it can
#        ever get.  Source differs because it REPLACES vertex lighting with the
#        cube plus up to four local lights; the per-pixel N.L against those is
#        what carves relief.  QC build 56 does not need any of it.

#   295  engine/client/in_generic.c, one default: in_journal_maxkb 8192 ->
#        262144.  No header change.  A .hid that hits the cap writes a
#        `truncated` marker and stops, which gives the run-submission rule a
#        THIRD state to carry -- "no journal, no rank" is one condition, and a
#        file that is present but stops early is neither present nor absent.
#        Every consumer would need a branch for it and the first time it fired
#        would be on somebody's long run.  256 MB is about 85 minutes at
#        ~1.15 MB/min plus the 293 'v' records, which no real run reaches.
#        Free to raise: in_jrn_cap starts at 64 KB and DOUBLES on demand bounded
#        by in_jrn_max, so the allocation tracks what is written.  What does
#        scale is the synchronous COM_WriteFile at run end (~40 ms at 8192), so
#        a journal that genuinely reached the new cap would hitch for over a
#        second -- only on a run where truncation would have been worse.

#   299  plugins/hl2/glsl/vmt/*.glsl (eight programs) plus one cvar in
#        mod_vbsp.c.  No engine change, but the GLSL header must be regenerated

#        FOG THE COLOUR, LEAVE THE ALPHA TO THE BLENDER.  Every VMT program
#        ended in fog4(diffuse_f), and fog4() is
#            vec4(fog3(rgb), 1.0) * regularcolour.a        gl_vidcommon.c:1887
#        -- it multiplies the fogged colour by the surface's own alpha.  That is
#        right for a PREMULTIPLIED blend and this plugin emits none: mat_vmt.c
#        :3719/:3885 write `src_alpha one_minus_src_alpha`, so GL was already
#        applying the alpha and fog4 applied it a SECOND time.
#        Source does not treat base alpha as opacity unless the material says
#        so.  On an opaque VertexLitGeneric it is a MASK -- $basealphaenvmapmask
#        and friends -- and the surface is solid.  With no fog the opaque blend
#        discards it, so nothing shows; the moment fog is on it multiplies every
#        pixel.  THAT is the reporter's "bright black" buildings out the
#        surf_tensor2 window: mean base alpha of the eight building materials in
#        frame splits 0.028-0.094 against 0.920-0.984, and it matches the screen
#        object for object -- the 0.03 props (row_hill_cluster, row_upscale)
#        drew at 3% of the fog colour, a black cutout against a sky that was
#        itself correctly fogged, and the low-alpha window panes inside the
#        0.92-0.96 textures are the black windows beside them.
#        hl2_fog_alphamul, default 0 = fixed; 1 restores fog4() bit-for-bit.
#        VERIFIED 2026-09-12, cfg/testrun/p299_verify.cfg: the two restore arms

#        written through INS_EnumerateDevices (a declared backend API every
#        backend implements) rather than by reaching into in_win.c's statics.
#        TWO TABLES ON PURPOSE: devids are allocated LAZILY at first use, so the
#        opening one answers "what hardware was present" and the closing one
#        "which device owns which devid"; a device in one and not the other was
#        plugged or unplugged mid-run.  `-` and `unset` are NOT 0, and 0 is a
#        real devid owned by a real device.

#        unbounded, that buffer is 1024 bytes), which edited the exact literal
#        IN_JournalSynth_f's fixed-width strstr poke depends on -- so arm B

#   305  engine/client/{in_generic.c,cl_input.c,input.h} (one declaration, no
#        struct change), plus tools/hidcheck.py.  THE YAW IDENTITY FAILED 57.67%
#        OF AN HONEST RUN, and the check was wrong rather than the run.
#        Lex's first real recording (bhop_eazy 0004976_pb, a clean PB, 42,973
#        'v' records) made Patch 293's identity print "YAW IDENTITY BROKEN on
#        22,844 of 42,972 frames".  The residual is BIMODAL with an empty
#        three-decade gap: no turn key held -> median 2.0e-6 deg; +left/+right
#        held -> median 0.0558 and NOT ONE frame below 1e-4.  Sign matches
#        CL_AdjustAngles 8,433 times of 8,437; residual/(cl_yawspeed*elapsed)
#        has median 1.0014; and all 1,938 zero-count turning frames fall inside
#        a +left or +right hold, none outside.  PITCH IS THE CONTROL and it is
#        exact (median residual 0.000000000) because cl_pitchspeed is 0 -- so
#        the mouse path was never wrong, the RECORD was incomplete.
#        CL_AdjustAngles writes the keyboard turn into viewanglechange[YAW],
#        the SAME accumulator IN_Move adds the mouse to, and nothing logged it.
#        NOT AN EDGE CASE: +left/+right are shipped default binds
#        (cfg/default.cfg:794,805) and this file's OWN prestrafe essay at
#        default.cfg:409-425 derives pm_groundspeed 260 from turning with them.
#        Keyboard turn is the prestrafe; the prestrafe opens every bhop run.
#        The 'v' line gains kpitch/kyaw, captured by DIFFING viewanglechange
#        across the CL_AdjustAngles call rather than re-deriving it -- a reader
#        would otherwise have to re-implement CL_KeyState's sub-frame fractions,
#        cl_anglespeedkey, in_strafe, in_rotate, r_xflip and the FPD_LIMIT_YAW
#        clamp, and would still break when any of them changed.
#        VERIFIED 2026-09-12, cfg/testrun/p305kbd.cfg -- and this is the rare
#        input patch a cfg can fully test, because +left/+right are COMMANDS:
#        arm B (+left 1.5s) sums kyaw +89.94 against cl_yawspeed 60 * 1.5 = 90,
#        arm C -90.30, arm A exactly 0, kpitch 0 in all three.  THE CONTROL is
#        arm B's own file with the kyaw column forced to 0: 144 of 144 turning
#        frames become unexplained, against 0 with it -- so the column does the
#        work and did not merely weaken the check.
#        hidcheck.py also had TWO defects this exposed: EPS was a flat 2e-6,
#        BELOW the float32 ulp of viewangles (1.526e-5 at 180 deg), which alone
#        produced 12,751 of the 22,844 faults; and check_identity sat under an
#        `if not frames: return`, so it never ran on the very files this patch
#        produces.  A pre-305 file now gets a NOTE, not a fault: absent is not
#        zero, and a residual there is unattributable rather than damning.
#   306  engine/client/{in_generic.c,in_win.c,client.h} (two extern int, no
#        struct change), plus tools/{hidcheck.py,test_hidcheck.py}.  RAW INPUT
#        WAS ALREADY REJECTING SYNTHESIZED EVENTS AND DELETING THE ONLY
#        EVIDENCE THAT IT HAD.  INS_RawInput_MouseRead drops any report whose
#        header.hDevice is not in the enumerated table -- that drop is why an
#        injected SendInput motion never reaches the view -- and it was silent,
#        so a journal from a clean session and one from a session where 40,000
#        synthesized events were thrown away were BYTE-IDENTICAL.
#        Two counters, never one: hDevice == NULL is synthesized input, while a
#        REAL handle not in the table is a mouse hot-plugged after init or an
#        RDP device excluded by in_rawinput_rdp -- innocent, and also a live
#        usability bug since that device's motion goes nowhere.  Collapsing
#        them would let "you hot-plugged a mouse" read as "you were attacked".
#        -1 IS NOT ZERO, the same rule Patch 301 set: -1 means this backend
#        does not count, 0 is a measurement.  New 'i <dt> <inj> <unenum>'
#        record, emitted when either moves, with the same honesty as the '!'
#        drop marker (the count is known, the TIME IS NOT).  Emitted from
#        IN_Journal_View as well as the drain, and ABOVE its quiet-frame
#        return -- a rejected report produces no event to drain, so an

#   312  engine/client/{in_generic.c,input.h} only, plus
#        tools/{hidcheck.py,test_hidcheck.py}.  Plan item P294b.
#        THE YAW IDENTITY TESTS THE ENGINE'S MULTIPLICATION, NOT THE PLAYER.
#        Patch 293 proves dyaw == -m_yaw*sens*scale*mx exactly -- but it
#        computes BOTH SIDES from the engine's own mx, so a hook that rewrites
#        the accumulated delta UPSTREAM of mx gets a conforming 293 record for
#        free: the engine then multiplies the doctored number correctly.  That
#        is the FTE shape of what momentum.dll does to CInput::ApplyMouse, and
#        293's own essay says so.  The 'm' records come off the event ring in
#        IN_Commands; mx is read from mouse->delta[] in IN_MoveMouse.  Those
#        are the two ends of exactly the window such a hook occupies, so a
#        DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN THEM IS THAT HOOK.
#        MEASURED BEFORE ANY CODE, because the plan required it (assuming a
#        frame correspondence is the mistake that made the .hid/.view
#        cross-check impossible): across every journal in the tree, 45,447 'v'
#        records including one real 4976-tick PB, the join ALREADY HELD on
#        45,384 of 45,384 covered frames, 100.000000%.  So 'v' needed no
#        sequence number and this patch adds no join key.
#        WHAT IT CATCHES, demonstrated on a real file.  Applying THE FAITHFUL
#        HOOK -- v.dx +1 AND yaw -k, which is what a real hook produces --
#          identity  exact on all 1 governed frames   <- 293 sees NOTHING
#          FAULT     COUNTS JOIN BROKEN ... sum(m)=(90,-2) but raw=(91,-2)
#        One count in ninety.  At scale on the same PB, a hook touching 93.68%
#        of frames moves 293's median residual from 0.121000 to 0.121001 deg
#        -- the SIXTH decimal -- and this check from 0.00% to 93.68%.
#        THREE THINGS HAD TO BE RECORDED FIRST, each of which would have
#        ACCUSED AN HONEST PLAYER (the Patch 305 failure mode):
#          in_xflip -- `mx *= -1` upstream of both taps, recorded NOWHERE.
#            Arm B: identical input, v.dx -157.5 vs the control's +157.5, with
#            no key in the header to say why.  Not CVAR_CHEAT, not archived.
#          counts discarded while the cursor is free -- mx zeroed before the
#            tap, so the quiet-frame return dropped the 'v' entirely while the
#            'm' records were already written, ORPHANING them into the next
#            window.  Arm D: one console press orphaned one 29-count batch.
#          the M_TOUCH path's hardcoded `mx *= 1.75`, found as an unexplained
#            ratio in the residuals before the line was located.
#        ONE TAP, NOT A TABLE OF EXEMPTIONS.  A red-team pass found SEVEN more
#        transforms between the 'm' record and 293's tap, six silent on an
#        honest client -- the Key_Dest_Has(~kdm_game) zeroing (whose dests are
#        NOT all covered by flags bit 4: opening chat discards counts with
#        flags reading 0), CSQC_MouseMove (on by default, and this game ships
#        CSQC panels), the two menu consumers, the weapon wheel (true on the
#        key alone regardless of threshold) and the touch boost.  ALL SIX ARE
#        DOWNSTREAM OF THE SINGLE TAP, so one line covers them and covers the
#        seventh nobody has written yet; a flag per discard site would need six
#        correct edits and fail silently when someone adds a seventh.
#        A SPARSE RECORD CANNOT MARK A SPAN -- fifth time after 301/306/307/310.
#        The obvious design marked touch mode with the 'a' record, but Patch 202
#        DEDUPES unchanged absolute positions, so a run spent ungrabbed carries
#        ONE 'a' for a state lasting the whole file: it would have exempted one
#        window and faulted every other.  The final run proved the replacement
#        by accident -- the launcher left the window unfocused, every frame took
#        the 1.75 boost (d 90 -2 against v 157.5 -3.5), and the join still
#        closed, because it is made against the value the engine STATED.
#        REJECTED, so it is not re-proposed: the first 'v' of a file is short
#        because in_journal_begin does not clear the pending ptr[].delta, and
#        clearing it would make the join exact from line one -- by THROWING AWAY
#        COUNTS THE PLAYER ALREADY MADE.  The engine must not alter a player's
#        aim, even by one frame, to tidy its own evidence.
#        WHAT IT DOES NOT COVER: an edit to the EVENT RING before the drain is
#        transcribed by the 'm' record as truth, so both ends agree; same for
#        the _GRID GetRawInputData pointer, a single static and so a data write
#        rather than a code patch.  Anyone who can hook IN_Journal_Raw authors
#        the whole file.  This spans the ring read to the delta read and claims
#        nothing outside it -- and it is COMPLEMENTARY to 293, not a
#        replacement: a hook that faithfully re-emits the counts and mutates
#        only the angle passes this and fails 293 instead.
#        Also enrols 293's own constants (sensitivity, m_yaw, m_filter, the
#        m_accel family) in the existing modifiedcount tracking -- they were
#        header snapshots, so a mid-run change stopped 293 closing with nothing

#        r_xflip, which inverts 293's sign downstream: the same alias trap 310

#   313  engine/server/{sv_phys.c,sv_init.c,sv_ccmds.c} and
#        engine/common/world.h (one declaration).  Plan item P296, the last
#        open item in the anti-cheat plan's Phase 1.
#        QC WAS STAMPING RUNS WITH HALF A PREDICATE.  sh_defs.qc's TF_CHEAT
#        comment had said so for builds: the authoritative test for "were the
#        physics locked" is SV_MovementLocked() = pm_lockmovement.defaultstr
#        && !sv_cheats, and QC can see only the second half.  It may NOT
#        re-derive the first from cvar("pm_lockmovement") -- that reads the
#        LIVE value, and Patch 170 reads defaultstr precisely because the live
#        one can be changed by the thing being policed.  So TF_CHEAT meant
#        "cheats were off", not "the ruleset was locked", and a server whose
#        pm_lockmovement default is 0 stamps every run on it clean.
#        Publishes *ruleset "<locked> <off> <seq> <breach>" into svs.info,
#        which SSQC reads straight back with serverkey() -- no network, no
#        clone.
#          locked  SV_MovementLocked(), BOTH halves.
#          off     how many cvars in pms_lockedmovevars[] currently differ
#                  from SV_MovementCanonical().  0 by construction while
#                  locked; carries everything when the lock is off.
#          seq     incremented on every set of a table cvar to an off-canonical
#                  value, WHETHER OR NOT THE LOCK IS ENGAGED.  Never reset.
#          breach  the subset of those that STOOD -- the lock was off, so
#                  nothing reverted them.  The ONLY field a taint may read.
#        WHY A COUNTER WHEN off EXISTS.  off is a snapshot and a snapshot
#        cannot mark a span: a cvar moved and moved back inside a run reads 0
#        at both ends.  Sixth instance of the same lesson (301, 306, 307, 310,
#        312).  ARM C measured it -- sv_cheats 1 -> "0 0 1 0", move ->
#        "0 1 2 1", sv_cheats 0 -> "1 0 2 1".  At the two ends of that window
#        off says nothing happened and breach says it did.  Counting sits ABOVE
#        the SV_MovementLocked test (the 306 shape), and is safe against the
#        bounded re-entry because Cvar_ForceSet's second pass arrives already
#        equal to canon and so neither counts nor publishes.
#        AND WHY TWO COUNTERS, which is the correction this patch took before
#        its consumer shipped.  seq counts ATTEMPTS; under the lock an attempt
#        is reverted inside the same Cvar_Set, so no physics tick ever sees it
#        and the run's physics WERE the ruleset's.  Tainting on seq would set a
#        bit meaning "not provably the ruleset's" on a run where they provably
#        were, because somebody mistyped a cvar name -- the Patch 305 failure
#        mode one layer up.  Narrowing seq was wrong too: a refused attempt is
#        real evidence and its console line is not parseable by anything.  So
#        both are published.  ARM B is that arm: "1 0 0 0" -> "1 0 1 0", seq
#        moves and breach does not.
#        THE COUNTER IS NOT REDUNDANT WITH QC's OWN sv_cheats WATCH, checked
#        before adding a second mechanism for one gesture.  That watch is an
#        EDGE (sv_player.qc:472) sampled once per PlayerPostThink, so a toggle
#        that goes 1 and back to 0 between two of them sets no TF_CHEAT while
#        every snapshot of this key still reads locked 1 / off 0.  Measured in
#        the arm built to isolate it, all three commands in one command-buffer
#        flush:
#          class:   clean (seg 0 cheat 0 cheatlatch 0)  cheats now 0
#          ruleset: key "1 0 3 2"  breachat 1  noruleset 0  would 1
#        A run every existing mechanism calls clean, whose physics left the
#        ruleset, seen by nothing but the counter.
#        ARM D DECIDED WHETHER IT SHIPS.  mode_bhop.cfg overrides six cvars in
#        the locked table (sv_airaccelerate 1000 vs a defaultstr of 150).
#        Those ARE the ruleset on a bhop map.  Measured "1 0 2 1" with all six
#        rows live-equals-base: off is 0, NOT 6.  A census against defaultstr
#        would have refused every bhop run on a correct server -- the Patch 305
#        failure mode, the flagship check calling the game's own mechanic a
#        cheat.
#        "0" AND NOT "" FOR UNLOCKED, where *cheats uses "":
#        PF_infokey_Internal falls through to svs.localinfo on an empty value
#        (pr_cmds.c:6478-6479), so "" would read as "go ask localinfo"; and the
#        key being ABSENT has to stay readable as its own third state, "this
#        engine predates 313 and cannot answer".  That is the 306 distinction.
#        NOT COVERED, said out loud: pm_lockmovement's own defaultstr.  A
#        second exec of a doctored default.cfg followed by the automatic
#        cvar_lockdefaults 1 (cmd.c:1067-1070) rewrites canonical under the
#        whole table with no callback anywhere.  That is console access to the
#        server process -- T3 -- which no patch in this series reaches.  Progs
#        can overwrite any star key too (PF_setserverkey), so the key is worth
#        exactly what the server's progs are worth: on the Ranked tier that is
#        the point, on a listen server it is nothing and so is everything else.
#        THE CONSUMER SHIPPED WITH IT, QC build 62: TF_NORULESET (1024), on the
#        TF_NOJOURNAL precedent -- recorded, read by the archive, implying none
#        of the three taint classes -- and NOT folded into SV_CheatsOn().  An
#        unlocked ruleset is a hole in the certification, not something the
#        player did, and folding it into TF_CHEAT would print `cheated` in red
#        at a player whose install was merely misconfigured.
#        SV_RulesetBroken is a PURE predicate called by both the latch
#        (SV_TimerRuleset, start and finish) and `cmd timer`'s report, so there
#        is one copy of the rule and asking does not mark the run.  That split
#        is forced by the harness: a run CANNOT BE FINISHED from a config on a
#        zoned map (zone_goto/setpos both void via SV_TimerWarped), so without
#        a live `would` column the finish half of the latch would not be
#        observable headlessly at all -- build 58's lesson, add a report line

#        plugins/hl2/mod_vbsp.c (one token).  Plan item: the BSP half of
#        Layer 1's "csprogs and BSP hashes pinned for ranked".
#        THE MAP HASH THE SERVER COMPARES WAS NEVER THE MAP'S.  sv_mapcheck
#        has defaulted 1 since QuakeWorld and kicks a client whose map hash
#        disagrees, so every audit read the cvar and moved on.  It had never
#        fired: VBSP_LoadModel computes the hash into `mod`, which the
#        submodel loop above reassigns, so it landed on the LAST SUBMODEL and
#        the world model kept Mod_FindName's memset zero.  Both ends did it,
#        so 0 == 0 agreed and every client passed.  It did not fail -- it was
#        INERT, which looks identical from outside.
#        Measured before fixing: 1287 of the library's 1312 maps have more
#        than one submodel and took the broken path; the 25 with exactly one
#        never entered the loop and had been correct all along, so a spot
#        check could have confirmed either answer.
#        Adds one infokey key with two subjects, neither on the wire:
#          infokey(world,  "*mapcrc")  the hash THIS SERVER loaded
#          infokey(player, "*mapcrc")  the hash THAT CLIENT claimed
#        Deliberately NOT in svs.info, unlike *csprogs: serverinfo reaches
#        every client and a patched one would only have to echo it back.
#        Hex strings, compared with strcmp -- a 32-bit checksum has no exact
#        QC float, and a numeric compare would call differing maps equal in
#        the direction that RANKS.  A zero checksum reports "" (unknown), not
#        the hash 0, which is what stops a patched server demoting every
#        still-unpatched client during the release window.
#        Consumed by QC build 73's TF_NOMAP (4096) and by a new `mapcrc` line
#        in the .rec header.  cfg/lobby.cfg gains `set sv_mapcheck 0` as the
#        gate: left at 1, the fleet stops accepting EVERY player the moment
#        the patched plugin lands, not just the ones with a wrong map.
#
#   NOTE: 314-320 are applied in the tree and written up in ENGINE_PATCHES.md
#        but are not enumerated here; several are another session's.  `patch`
#        above is the highest APPLIED number, which is what it has always
#        meant.
#
#   322  engine/server/sv_ccmds.c (a console command) plus a comment-only
#        block in engine/common/com_bih.c.  NO behaviour change -- this one is
#        an INSTRUMENT.  Plan experiment E4.
#        CAN THE MOVER LEAVE THE MACHINE IT WAS BUILT ON?  Phase 3 wants a
#        verifier that RE-COMPUTES a submitted time, which rests on the mover
#        being deterministic; client prediction agreeing with the server proves
#        that on ONE BINARY and is routinely cited as if it proved more.
#        pm_dettest [traces] [ticks] drives a fixed, integer-derived input set
#        through three things and prints THREE hashes, because "they differ" is
#        not a finding and the three suspects need different remedies:
#          libm   sin/cos/atan2/sqrt over 4096 fixed inputs
#          trace  4096 traces against the world model -- no libm, no mover
#          mover  2048 ticks of PM_PlayerMove, world-only physents
#        Measured x86-64/msvcrt against aarch64/glibc, same source, same plugin:
#          ANSWER: YES, and it costs one compiler flag.  With -ffp-contract=off
#          on aarch64 the mover is BIT-IDENTICAL over 2048 ticks.  Without it,
#          velocity drifts one ulp from tick 1 -- aarch64 has FMA in its
#          baseline so gcc's default -ffp-contract=fast fuses, x86-64 does not.
#          libm AGREES, which is the opposite of what the plan assumed: in-tree
#          trig is not on Phase 3's critical path.
#          RESIDUAL: the trace hash still differs.  Not contraction, not libm,
#          not patches 317/318/320 (all controlled for).  It is the BIH sort --
#          BIH_Sort_X/Y/Z return `am > bm`, never negative.  The obvious fix was
#          tried on both arms and REVERTED: it changes which surface wins a tie
#          (physics, standing records), glibc was already unaffected by the bug,
#          and the two still disagreed, because qsort is UNSTABLE and those
#          comparators return 0 for equal keys.  Closing it needs a TOTAL order,
#          not a sign.  Left in place with the whole analysis at the site.
#
#   327  engine/server/sv_ccmds.c only (a console command beside pm_dettest).
#        NO behaviour change -- the second INSTRUMENT in this file.  Plan
#        experiment E3, and the last of the three Phase 0 experiments to become
#        answerable: until QC build 82 put a usercmd stream in the .rec there was
#        nothing to replay, because every recording was a STATE trace.
#        DOES A RECORDED INPUT TRACE REPLAY?  pm_recsim <file.rec> feeds the `in`
#        records back through the mover that produced them and measures three
#        things, the first of which needs no geometry at all:
#          1 tick arithmetic -- the mover's own ticksrun against the file's
#            movetick delta, i.e. the derived duration tested against pm_source.c
#          2 pinned loop -- org/vel snapped to the sample each packet, so errors
#            cannot compound and the number means ONE packet of divergence
#          3 open loop -- seeded once, never corrected; what a verifier does
#        Measured on a real, clean (flags 0) bhop run of 1340 rows:
#          ANSWER: YES, to the limit of what the file can express.  1339/1339
#          moves exact on arm 1.  1307 of 1324 packets at the file's own PRINTING
#          FLOOR on arm 2 -- origin error median 0.0046 u against a %.2f sample,
#          i.e. +-0.005 u is the floor and the residue is the rounding.
#          SO BUILD 82'S REFUSAL OF A FRAMETIME COLUMN HOLDS, and %.5f on <carry>
#          survives the round trip -- neither was obvious from the arithmetic.
#          WHAT IT FOUND is the fifteen packets that do NOT reproduce, and none
#          of them is a simulation failure:
#            TEN ARE TELEPORTS -- ~250 u of position with velocity PRESERVED
#            exactly, i.e. trigger_teleport (sv_entities.qc:4632, stage/bonus
#            teleports and the "you fell off" resets).  The .rec records the
#            CONSEQUENCE and never the EVENT, so a verifier cannot tell one from
#            a cheat that moved the player, and an open loop dies at the first.
#            trigger_push and trigger_setspeed are the same class via velocity.
#            TWO are one-tick jump-edge disagreements at a landing.
#          ALSO: the FINAL move's duration is underivable (1340 rows, 1339
#          durations, and the missing one is the move the finish latches on), and
#          the file pins the map and the zones but not the movevars, the player
#          hull or pm_type.
#        NEGATIVE CONTROL: build 82's own recording, which is half a noclip
#        flight.  pm_type is not in the format, so the sim collides where the
#        flight did not and reports >1000 u/s from the row that typed `noclip` --
#        a tool that reproduced both files equally well would measure nothing.
#
#   328  engine/server/sv_ccmds.c only (the same console command).  NO behaviour
#        change.  The reader half of QC build 83, which writes the event 327
#        proved was missing: a `warp` record per imposed state change, and a
#        `startjit` header key for the randomized start.
#          THE PARSER HAD NO else-BRANCH, and that was the real finding here.  It
#          knew samples and `in` rows; an unrecognised record was not skipped
#          with a count, it was INVISIBLE, because the two-pass allocator counted
#          only the two types it knew.  The tool that found the teleport problem
#          could not have seen the record written to fix it.
#          `warp` is now parsed and APPLIED IN BOTH ARMS -- it is evidence the
#          FILE states, not a correction the HARNESS makes, and arm 2 already
#          papered over teleports by snapping to the sample every packet, which
#          is why its numbers looked fine in 327 while the format was short.
#          `startjit` is read and REPORTED and never applied: the first sample is
#          already post-displacement, so the seed carries it, and the key's job
#          is to explain the 2 u step between the last padding sample and the
#          first run sample rather than leave it reading as physics.
#        MEASURED with a control REQUIRED TO FAIL (cfg/testrun/b83run.cfg):
#                                SUBJECT v7        CONTROL v6
#          warps                 10                0
#          arm 1 tick arithmetic 1749/1749 exact   1339/1339 exact
#          arm 2 median          0.0049 u          0.0046 u
#          ARM 3 OPEN LOOP DIES  row 817           row 149
#          The control is data/e3trace.rec, the build-82 file 327 was measured
#          on.  The open loop crossed SIX trigger_teleports it could not
#          previously cross even one of; the control still dies at its first.
#          A harness that had merely got LOOSER would have improved both.
#          And the control reproduces 327's numbers to the digit under a
#          different binary, so 328 moved nothing it was not meant to.
#          WHAT STOPS IT NOW IS NOT A MISSING FACT: row 817 sits four rows before
#          a velocity error of 301.99 against a jumpvelocity of 301.993 -- one
#          tick of disagreement about when a landing became a jump, which is
#          chaos at a discrete boundary and which no format can record away.
#        RESIDUAL, RECORDED AND NOT EXPLAINED: three consecutive arm-2 packets
#        read an origin error of EXACTLY 16.00 u (the hull half-width) at a
#        landing.  NOT this patch -- those rows carry movetick 2084-2089 and the
#        nearest warps are at 1857 and 2168, so nothing was applied within 200
#        ticks of them, and the control shows none of it.  Cause unknown, and a
#        fourth explanation is not being offered.

#   484  engine/client/in_generic.c only (one function, five cvars, one call
#        site, one reset block), plus tools/{hidcheck.py,rcptcheck.py,
#        p484ident.py}, cfg/test/p484ident.cfg and surfd/{sweep,surfd,admin}.py.
#        THE INPUT IDENTITY WAS COMPUTED ONLY WHERE THE FILE IS, AND THE FILE

#        _skipped.  Per-journal, reset in IN_JournalBegin_f -- these belong in a
#        recording's header, and one that survived a journal begin would
#        attribute one run's ghosts to the next.

#        counts an angle was built from, and a rewrite that sits where the

#        keyboard turn behind it.  BELOW this line the two are indistinguishable,
#        ABOVE it the counts do not exist yet.  The same argument Patch 376 made
#        for the mouse counters.
#        THE ABSTENTIONS ARE THE PATCH.  A frame is not governed when the cursor
#        was free (flags & 4), when m_filter or m_accel is on, when sensitivity is
#        0, or when neither axis governed.  Per axis: strafe_y removes pitch,
#        strafe_x removes yaw, and A PITCH PREDICTION THAT WOULD LEAVE THE CLAMP
#        ENVELOPE IS SKIPPED AND COUNTED, NEVER MODELLED -- pitch is clamped
#        (cl_input.c) and yaw is not, and the bound is a SERVERINFO value the
#        file does not carry (cl_main.c reads minpitch/maxpitch out of
#        cl.serverinfo; the engine default is -70/+80, cfg/default.cfg sets

#        recorded at +-89 produced 910 pitch failures of which 892 WERE
#        PITCH-ONLY -- 892 false accusations on one honest PB, against 18 with
#        the bound read off the client.
#        COUNTED PER FRAME, NOT PER AXIS, and tools/p484ident.py Part 2 caught
#        the first cut summing them: over 113 journals every frame that failed did
#        so on BOTH axes (11 pitch, 11 yaw, the same 11 frames), so a per-axis
#        sum reports 22 for 11 events.  The per-axis split stays in the .hid;
#        the counters a server sees are per frame, which is the unit a gate
#        thresholds on.
#        SIGN MEASURED, NOT READ.  Sweeping all eight combinations of (angle
#        column, key column, count column, sign) over two PBs, exactly two fit at
#        ~0 failures -- pitch from dy with kpitch POSITIVE, yaw from dx with kyaw
#        NEGATIVE -- and the wrong sign gives 38,744 failures against 0.
#        hidcheck.py's own comment says the opposite ("The sign convention is the
#        OPPOSITE of what it looks like") and that comment is wrong; the check
#        was always right.  Recorded so the next reader does not re-derive it.
#        VERIFIED cfg/test/p484ident.cfg + tools/p484ident.py: 18 checks, 0
#        failed.  ARM A FAILED ON THE FIRST RUN AND THE FAILURE WAS THE FINDING:
#        ghosts and violations read 0 with no journal open, because the first cut
#        published them unguarded while frames/skipped were guarded on
#        `!count && !skip` -- an unmeasured state wearing a clean measurement's
#        clothes.  The guard is now a "journal has been opened" flag, because a
#        counter cannot say whether it has been measured, and a journal whose
#        frames were ALL skipped has run and governed nothing and must read 0.
#        ARM A2: all five refuse `set` ("variable ... is readonly") and still read
#        -1.  That is the property which makes the channel mean anything -- a

#        over the 113 local journals the honest corpus carries 11 violations on 3

#        3 files is a guess, and a check that faults an honest run is worse than
#        no check.  The gate is BACKLOG item C and needs a corpus, which needs

#   491  engine/server/sv_cluster.c.  A remote server could drop a connected client
#        and re-purpose its process into a cluster master: `mapcluster` (and

#        `char allskins[128]`, with the skin cache declared immediately after it, so
#        a stuffed 300-character name wrote 172 bytes past a global.  Now
#        `Q_strncpyz` -- the BOUND, not a gate, because forcing skins is a
#        legitimate server feature and must stay server-reachable.  Quantified by a
#        canary build (a guard word at offset 128 came back holding the argument's
#        own bytes, and the client died in the next statement), with the real
#        binaries graded separately and behaving identically before and after.
#        CLIENT-ONLY.  Arm: C:/FTESurf-private/poc/p489/ (private).
#   488  engine/client/renderer.c.  A server could name the DLL the client's
#        renderer loads: the explicit subrenderer token was stored unfiltered
#        while the path filter beside it ran only on the gl_driver fallback, and
#        that token goes to Sys_LoadLibrary (GL) or LoadLibrary (Vulkan).
#        `setrenderer` had no restriction level, so stufftext reached it -- a
#        canary DLL's DllMain ran in the client's pid.  Now refused whole from an
#        insecure caller; a BARE name is refused too, because LoadLibrary
#        resolves it through the search order including the client's cwd, so a
#        slash filter would have been the incomplete fix.  CLIENT-ONLY.
#        Arm: C:/FTESurf-private/poc/p488/ (private -- a working recipe for
#        native code execution that every shipped engine still has).
#   487  engine/client/zqtp.c, engine/common/cmd.c + cmd.h.  A trigger alias's
#        body was executed at RESTRICT_LOCAL with the alias's own `execlevel`
#        never consulted, so a server-planted `alias f_newmap "<anything>"` ran
#        with the user's privileges and every Cmd_IsInsecure() gate read 29 for
#        it -- the one hole that defeated all of 481/483/485 at once.  Now
#        resolved through Cmd_AliasExecLevel at both executing sites and both of
#        TP_ExecTrigger's branches; a locally defined alias falls back to
#        RESTRICT_LOCAL exactly as before, so the legitimate feature is
#        unchanged (measured identical on both builds).  CLIENT-ONLY: a
#        dedicated server never runs TP_ExecTrigger, so no lobby deploy.
#        Arm: C:/FTESurf-private/poc/p487/ (private -- a working recipe for a
#        hole every shipped engine still has, 0.1.22 = patch 467).
#
# Because the tree is shared, the deployed ftesurf64.exe contains all of them.
# Anyone bisecting a rendering or input change should look at 268 before
# assuming build 47's QC did it, and anyone comparing a framerate recorded
# before 273 against one recorded after should read 273's first bullet first:
# the number changed meaning, not just value.

# Build it (from that tree):
#   make -C engine  m-rel       FTE_TARGET=win64
#   make -C engine  sv-rel      FTE_TARGET=win64
#   make -C plugins/hl2                                  # regenerates the GLSL header
#   make -C engine  plugins-rel FTE_TARGET=win64 NATIVE_PLUGINS="hl2 cod box3d ode"
#   make -C engine  qcc-rel     FTE_TARGET=win64         # the QC compiler
#
# FTE_TARGET=win64 is mandatory -- msys2's gcc reports "mingw", so the
# auto-detect in engine/Makefile picks win32.  A full rebuild is required after
# ANY engine header change; the makefiles do not track header dependencies.


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  PROVENANCE OF src/defs/*.qc
#
#  Those three files (~361 KB) are not hand-written.  They are the output of
#  FTE's own pr_dumpplatform builtin -- declarations of the engine's builtins
#  and extensions -- and are regenerated rather than edited:
#
#      pr_dumpplatform -O csdefs -Tcs  -Ffte      -> cl_defs.qc
#      pr_dumpplatform -O svdefs -Tsv  -Ffte      -> sv_defs.qc
#      pr_dumpplatform -O mdefs  -Tmenu -Ffte     -> m_defs.qc
#
#  They were seeded from an earlier project of mine, github.com/ProtoAus/Nettest
#  (m_defs.qc is byte-identical to a blob committed there; sv_defs.qc is 99.3%
#  identical and cl_defs.qc about 95%), and have been extended since.
#
#  They describe a GPLv2 engine's internals, which is the strongest reason this
#  repository is GPLv2-or-later.  See LICENSE.


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  PROVENANCE OF THE SOURCE-ENGINE CITATIONS IN THE COMMENTS
#
#  The QuakeC and cfg/default.cfg cite Source and Momentum Mod C++ files by name
#  and line number in about 600 places.  Recording this once, here, so it does
#  not have to be re-litigated:
#
#    * Valve's files (gamemovement.cpp, triggers.cpp, baseentity.cpp, props.cpp,
#      viewrender.cpp, disp_vbsp.cpp and the rest) are from the PUBLIC
#      Source SDK 2013: github.com/ValveSoftware/source-sdk-2013
#
#    * Momentum's files (mom_gamemovement.cpp, mom_gamerules.cpp,
#      mom_system_gamemode.*, gamemovement_momentummod.cpp) are from
#      github.com/momentum-mod/game, which the Momentum team deliberately left
#      public under the Source 1 SDK Licence when development moved
#      closed-source in 2020.  Their README says so explicitly.
#
#  FTESurf reproduces the BEHAVIOUR those files document.  It contains no Valve
#  or Momentum code, and it ships no Source assets -- mounted Steam content is
#  read from the player's own installs at runtime.  The citations are there so
#  any value in this tree can be checked against the thing it had to match.
